## Aftermath

### Election night

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Claudia_Sheinbaum_discurso_de_la_victoria.jpg/500px-Claudia_Sheinbaum_discurso_de_la_victoria.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Claudia_Sheinbaum_discurso_de_la_victoria.jpg) Claudia Sheinbaum during her victory speech at the [Zócalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z%C3%B3calo "Zócalo").

At 11:50 PM CST on 2 June, the INE's president, [Guadalupe Taddei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadalupe_Taddei "Guadalupe Taddei"), declared that according to the INE's Quick Count, Claudia Sheinbaum was the winner of the presidential contest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-:14) The Quick Count also projected that [Sigamos Haciendo Historia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigamos_Haciendo_Historia "Sigamos Haciendo Historia") would keep its control of the [Congress of the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_the_Union "Congress of the Union"), with a [supermajority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermajority "Supermajority") in the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_(Mexico) "Chamber of Deputies (Mexico)") and a probable supermajority in the [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_the_Republic_(Mexico) "Senate of the Republic (Mexico)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-1)[\[225\]](#refnote-:14)

Shortly after the INE's announcement, [Xóchitl Gálvez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X%C3%B3chitl_G%C3%A1lvez "Xóchitl Gálvez") and [Jorge Máynez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_M%C3%A1ynez "Jorge Máynez") called Sheinbaum to congratulate her on her victory.[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-232) During their respective press conferences, both Gálvez and Máynez publicly conceded, with Máynez highlighting that the results represented the best performance for [Citizens' Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizens'_Movement_(Mexico) "Citizens' Movement (Mexico)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-233)[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-234) However, Gálvez later described the electoral campaign as an "unequal competition against the entire state apparatus dedicated to favoring its candidate", adding that she would challenge the result.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-235)

Reacting to the result on [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_(social_network) "X (social network)"), Sheinbaum said that she would not let the electorate down.[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-236) She later addressed her supporters at the [Zócalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z%C3%B3calo "Zócalo") in Mexico City, who had gathered at the plaza to celebrate her victory.[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-237) President [Andrés Manuel López Obrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Manuel_L%C3%B3pez_Obrador "Andrés Manuel López Obrador") described Sheinbaum's victory as historic and reiterated his pledge not to interfere in her incoming administration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-238)

### District tally and certification

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Resultados_electorales_presidencia_de_M%C3%A9xico_2024.jpg/500px-Resultados_electorales_presidencia_de_M%C3%A9xico_2024.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Resultados_electorales_presidencia_de_M%C3%A9xico_2024.jpg) [Andrés Manuel López Obrador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Manuel_L%C3%B3pez_Obrador "Andrés Manuel López Obrador") showing Sheinbaum's margin of victory in one of his _mañaneras_, 25 July 2024.

The district tally started on 5 June at 8:00 CST. The INE announced it would recount ballots from 60% of the polling stations, a decrease from the 75% recounted during the 2018 election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-239) Xóchitl Gálvez requested that 80% of the votes be recounted, asserting there were irregularities at some polling stations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-240)

On 6 June, the district tally concluded for the presidential election, increasing Sheinbaum's margin of victory by 0.85%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-241) On 9 June, the INE released constituency seats and final vote count results for each district.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:18-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-:18) Projections using the final vote count to calculate party-list deputies and senators confirmed a supermajority in the Chamber of Deputies but not in the Senate, falling three seats short of the two-thirds majority needed to change the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Mexico "Constitution of Mexico").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:18-1)[\[236\]](#refnote-:18)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:15-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-:15)

On 15 August, after all challenges to the presidential election were resolved, Sheinbaum's victory was officially certified, and she received the certificate designating her as president-elect.[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-244)[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-245)

On 21 August, the INE's Commission on Prerogatives and Political Parties unanimously approved a draft assigning party-list deputies and senators.[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-246)

### Party registrations

On 10 June, for the first time in the party's three-decade-long history, the INE notified the [Party of the Democratic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_the_Democratic_Revolution "Party of the Democratic Revolution") (PRD) that it had failed to meet 3% of the popular vote to keep its registration as a national political party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-247) On 19 June, an auditor was chosen to oversee the party's liquidation,[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-248) and on 19 September, the INE declared that the PRD had lost its registration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-249)

### Lawsuits

After the election, the [National Action Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Action_Party_(Mexico) "National Action Party (Mexico)") (PAN), the [Institutional Revolutionary Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutional_Revolutionary_Party "Institutional Revolutionary Party") (PRI), the [Party of the Democratic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_the_Democratic_Revolution "Party of the Democratic Revolution") (PRD), and [Citizens' Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizens'_Movement_(Mexico) "Citizens' Movement (Mexico)") (MC) collectively filed 240 challenges against the presidential election results. The PRD spearheaded most of these challenges, which sought to annul millions of ballots to meet the 3% threshold required to maintain its registration as a national political party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-250) However, by 8 August, the [Electoral Tribunal of the Federal Judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Electoral_Tribunal "Federal Electoral Tribunal") (TEPJF) had rejected all of the challenges.[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-251)

For the legislative elections, all political parties challenged the results for deputies and senators, citing allegations of exceeding campaign spending limits, vote buying, voter intimidation, and various irregularities in vote counting.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:17-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-:17) Most of these challenges, when presented to the Electoral Tribunal of the Federal Judiciary, were rejected, and any rulings from lower courts did not alter the overall election results.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:17-1)[\[246\]](#refnote-:17) The only exception was in the 23rd district of the [State of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Mexico "State of Mexico"), where Luis Alberto Carballo Gutiérrez's victory certificate was annulled due to his ineligibility, as he was a child support debtor,[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-253) resulting in his alternate assuming the position instead.[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-254)

The National Electoral Institute’s (INE) seat allocation for proportional representation seats in the Chamber of Deputies was challenged by opposition parties before the Electoral Tribunal of the Federal Judiciary (TEPJF).[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-255)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:22-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-:22) The challengers argued that the seat allocation violated the constitutional 8% cap on over-representation established in Article 54 of the Constitution, noting that the [Sigamos Haciendo Historia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigamos_Haciendo_Historia "Sigamos Haciendo Historia") coalition would hold approximately 73% of the seats with less than 60% of the national vote.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:22-1)[\[250\]](#refnote-:22) They contended that the over-representation limit should be applied to electoral coalitions as a whole rather than to individual parties.[&#8202;](#ref-back-257-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-257)\[ _[AI-retrieved source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template:AI-retrieved_source/doc "Template:AI-retrieved source/doc")_\] On 28 August 2024, the TEPJF rejected these challenges, reaffirming its jurisprudence that the 8% over-representation limit applies to individual political parties and not to coalitions. The tribunal ruled that the INE’s methodology was consistent with constitutional and statutory electoral law, citing precedents from its rulings following the [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Mexican_legislative_election "2015 Mexican legislative election") and [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Mexican_general_election "2018 Mexican general election") elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-258)[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-259)

### Reactions

#### Financial markets

Amid concerns that a [supermajority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermajority "Supermajority") in both chambers of Congress would lead to anti-market reforms, the [peso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_peso "Mexican peso") dropped nearly 4% against the U.S. dollar following initial reporting;[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-260) by the end of the week, it was down 10% from its pre-election level.[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-261) From less than 17 to the U.S. dollar on 2 June, the peso fell over the nine days following the election to trade at 18.50 on 11 June,[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-262) still considerably stronger than the all-time low of over 25 seen at the start of the COVID-19 pandemic in March 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-263)
On 3 June, the two largest [ETFs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange-traded_fund "Exchange-traded fund") focused on Mexico ( [iShares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IShares "IShares") MSCI Mexico ETF EWW and [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Templeton_Investments "Franklin Templeton Investments") FTSE Mexico ETF FLMX) dropped more than 10%, the biggest daily decline [in four years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Mexico "COVID-19 pandemic in Mexico").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Wang-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-Wang) The [Mexican Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Stock_Exchange "Mexican Stock Exchange") ended the 3 June trading day down 6.1%, while the [MSCI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSCI "MSCI") Mexico Index dropped 8.8%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Strohecker-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-Strohecker)

- ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Flag_of_Venezuela.svg/60px-Flag_of_Venezuela.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela "Venezuela"): President [Nicolás Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro "Nicolás Maduro") congratulated Sheinbaum, calling it a "victory for [the great homeland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patria_Grande "Patria Grande")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-292)

## Notes

1. [&#8202;](#refnote-3)[↑](#ref-back-3-0)Gálvez, while not officially affiliated with the PAN, secured the nomination with the party's support as an external candidate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-1-0)[\[1\]](#refnote-1) Additionally, according to the coalition agreement, the constituent parties of _Fuerza y Corazón por México_ recognize the presidential nominee as part of the PAN.[&#8202;](#ref-back-2-0)[\[2\]](#refnote-2)
2. [&#8202;](#refnote-4)[↑](#ref-back-4-0)There were instances in some states where individual parties ran without being apart of a coalition.
3. [&#8202;](#refnote-227)[↑](#ref-back-227-0)Of the elected candidates, 25 belong to the National Regeneration Movement, 7 to the Ecologist Green Party of Mexico, and 7 to the Labor Party.
4. [&#8202;](#refnote-228)[↑](#ref-back-228-0)Of the elected candidates, 15 belong to the National Action Party, 12 to the Institutional Revolutionary Party, and 2 to the Party of the Democratic Revolution.
5. [&#8202;](#refnote-229)[↑](#ref-back-229-0)Of the elected candidates, 124 belong to the National Regeneration Movement, 57 to the Ecologist Green Party of Mexico, and 38 to the Labor Party.
6. [&#8202;](#refnote-230)[↑](#ref-back-230-0)Of the elected candidates, 29 belong to the National Action Party, 9 to the Institutional Revolutionary Party, and 1 to the Party of the Democratic Revolution.
